4 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, stereovision1.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dspcplx_mult_re (precision INT18)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSPCPLX block in one configuration, "18 x 18 Complex MULT/MACC, real half (DSP_MODE = CINT18)" (AM004 v1.2.1 Table 6 (p43), chapter 5 (p76-85), register combinations Tables 22 and 23 (p83-85)). It is not the whole DSPCPLX: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (C_RE = 0, ALUMODE_RE = 0000, CARRYINSEL_RE = 000, CARRYIN_RE = 0, ASYNC_RST = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one tile holds one half of the unit; AMD's unit is two DSP58s that receive the operands once; the C_RE port is not exposed; OPMODE_RE is restricted to the listed values; the cascade, carry and pattern-detect outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P_RE = A_RE*B_RE - A_IM*B_IM; in full, with ai = CONJUGATE_A ? -A_IM : A_IM and bi = CONJUGATE_B ? -B_IM : B_IM, M <= A_RE*B_RE - ai*bi. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE_RE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A_RE[18], A_IM[18], B_RE[18], B_IM[18], CONJUGATE_A[1], CONJUGATE_B[1], OPMODE_RE[9], RSTM_RE[1], RSTP_RE[1], CEM_RE[1], CEP_RE[1]
  • output ports: P_RE[58]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each 18-bit operand must be zero (AM004 p62, as for every DSP58 multiplier input). So an unsigned operand may be at most 17 bits on A_RE, 17 bits on A_IM, 17 bits on B_RE and 17 bits on B_IM; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. P_RE is a 58-bit value accumulated modulo 2^58. The result wraps at 58 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 58 bits (modulo 2^58), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. M loads the real part of a*b from the operand and CONJUGATE pins at a clock edge where CEM_RE is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTM_RE. P_RE loads the value OPMODE_RE selects from M and P_RE at a clock edge where CEP_RE is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP_RE; the output P_RE is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A_RE, A_IM, B_RE, B_IM, CONJUGATE_A and CONJUGATE_B reach P_RE 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P_RE after edge k+1); OPMODE_RE has no register before the one that drives P_RE: the value present at a clock edge is in P_RE after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE_RE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P_RE. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P_RE <= M
    • 000100101 (accumulate): P_RE <= P_RE + M
    • 010000000 (hold): P_RE <= P_RE
    • 000000000 (zero): P_RE <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P_RE under it leaves P_RE unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • control CONJUGATE_A: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts with the operands, at the clock edge that loads M. 1 negates A_IM before the multiply (a = A_RE - j A_IM). 0 and 1 are both legal.
  • control CONJUGATE_B: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts with the operands, at the clock edge that loads M. 1 negates B_IM before the multiply (b = B_RE - j B_IM). 0 and 1 are both legal.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTM_RE and RSTP_RE; clock enables CEM_RE and CEP_RE): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P_RE is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE_RE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at RSTM_RE, RSTP_RE, CEM_RE and CEP_RE: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at A_RE, A_IM, B_RE, B_IM, CONJUGATE_A, CONJUGATE_B and OPMODE_RE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P_RE (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P_RE (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into A_RE, A_IM, B_RE, B_IM, CONJUGATE_A, CONJUGATE_B and OPMODE_RE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P_RE, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

Multiplies in the unmodified stereovision1, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 152 on 41 DSP tiles, all in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode (35 tiles with 4, 2 with 3, 2 with 2 and 2 with 1). The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle controls, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTM_RE and RSTP_RE), clock enables (CEM_RE and CEP_RE) and per-cycle controls (OPMODE_RE, CONJUGATE_A and CONJUGATE_B) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).

Output rules

What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate's plan

REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78, corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79, corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104, corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr_out_tmp in corr (line 1385) and in corr_seq (line 1869) — the sum lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg, which the block's M stage computes directly
BLOCKS: 76 (one per instance: 64 of corr, 12 of corr_seq)
MAPPING: per corr instance — A_RE = {10'b0, in_l_re}, B_RE = {10'b0, in_r_re}, A_IM = {10'b0, in_l_im}, B_IM = {10'b0, in_r_im}, CONJUGATE_A = 1'b1, CONJUGATE_B = 1'b0, OPMODE_RE = 9'b000000101, CEM_RE = 1'b1, CEP_RE = 1'b1, RSTM_RE = 1'b0, RSTP_RE = 1'b0, CLK = clk, P_RE[15:0] replaces corr_out_tmp. Per corr_seq instance — identical, but the operands are the existing soft registers: A_RE = {10'b0, in_l_re_reg}, B_RE = {10'b0, in_r_re_reg}, A_IM = {10'b0, in_l_im_reg}, B_IM = {10'b0, in_r_im_reg}, CLK = clk, P_RE[15:0] replaces corr_out_tmp.
PLAN:

Why the block fits. With CONJUGATE_A = 1 and CONJUGATE_B = 0 the block's M stage computes A_RE*B_RE - (-A_IM)*B_IM = A_RE*B_RE + A_IM*B_IM, which is exactly in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im — the whole reduction that corr (lines 1374, 1375, 1385) and corr_seq (lines 1867, 1868, 1869) perform. Both multiplies of a module and the adder that sums them collapse into one block, so each of the 76 module instances needs exactly one block.

Visibility check. In corr, lrexrre_reg (1367) and limxrim_reg (1368) are read only at line 1385, and corr_out_tmp (1369) only at line 1379. In corr_seq, lrexrre_reg (1844) and limxrim_reg (1845) are read only at line 1869, corr_out_tmp (1846) only at line 1856. Nothing else inside or outside these modules reads them, so they may all live inside the block. corr_out is a module output and stays in soft logic. in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg in corr_seq (1840-1843) are read at 1860-1863 (their own hold path) besides the multiplies, so they stay in soft logic exactly as written and only feed the block's operand pins. (The four same-named wires in corr, lines 1363-1366, are undriven and unread; leave them alone.)

Arithmetic check. All four operands are 8-bit unsigned, so they are zero-extended to 18 bits and the top bit is 0 as the block requires (8 ≤ 17). Each original product is at most 65025 and is stored in a 16-bit register without truncation; their sum is then truncated to 16 bits by corr_out_tmp. The block computes the sum exactly in 58 bits, so P_RE[15:0] equals (in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im) mod 2^16 — bit-identical to the original corr_out_tmp. No wrap of P_RE occurs. No term is dropped: both multiplies and the add are block terms; nothing is left outside.

Registers and reset. Neither module has a reset, so RSTM_RE = RSTP_RE = 0; both pipeline stages run unconditionally in the original, so CEM_RE = CEP_RE = 1 and OPMODE_RE is the constant 9'b000000101 (multiply). Original registers start at 0 and the block's M and P_RE start at 0, so cycles 1 and 2 after power-up match too (original: corr_out_tmp loads 0+0 = 0 at the first edge; block: P_RE loads M = 0). corr_out keeps its soft enabled register driven by new_data.

Register stages, before and after.

  • corr (candidates :1374$78 and :1375$79), before: operands → stage 1 lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg → stage 2 corr_out_tmp → stage 3 corr_out (enabled by new_data). After: operands → stage 1 block M → stage 2 block P_RE (= corr_out_tmp) → stage 3 soft corr_out. Three stages before and after; the block's 2-cycle operand-to-P_RE latency matches the two stages it absorbs.
  • corr_seq (candidates :1867$104 and :1868$105), before: inputs → stage 1 in_*_reg (enabled by new_data) → stage 2 lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg → stage 3 corr_out_tmp → stage 4 corr_out. After: inputs → stage 1 soft in_*_reg (unchanged) → stage 2 block M → stage 3 block P_RE (= corr_out_tmp) → stage 4 soft corr_out. Four stages before and after.

No candidate region is left unconverted; all four are converted.

Edits to make (do not change anything else).

  1. In module corr (lines 1352-1387):

    • Delete the declarations of lrexrre_reg (1367), limxrim_reg (1368) and corr_out_tmp (1369), and delete lines 1374, 1375 and 1385.

    • Add wire [57:0] p_re; and the instance:

       dspcplx_mult_re u_cplx (
     .CLK(clk),
     .A_RE({{(18-sh_reg_w){1'b0}}, in_l_re}),
     .A_IM({{(18-sh_reg_w){1'b0}}, in_l_im}),
     .B_RE({{(18-sh_reg_w){1'b0}}, in_r_re}),
     .B_IM({{(18-sh_reg_w){1'b0}}, in_r_im}),
     .CONJUGATE_A(1'b1), .CONJUGATE_B(1'b0),
     .OPMODE_RE(9'b000000101),
     .RSTM_RE(1'b0), .RSTP_RE(1'b0),
     .CEM_RE(1'b1), .CEP_RE(1'b1),
     .P_RE(p_re));
    • The remaining always block keeps only the new_data branch, with corr_out <= p_re[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]; on the taken branch and corr_out <= corr_out; otherwise.

  2. In module corr_seq (lines 1829-1871):

    • Keep lines 1850-1865 exactly as they are (the four enabled operand registers and the corr_out load).
    • Delete the declarations of lrexrre_reg (1844), limxrim_reg (1845) and corr_out_tmp (1846), and delete lines 1867, 1868 and 1869.
    • Add wire [57:0] p_re; and the same instance, with A_RE/A_IM/B_RE/B_IM driven by in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg zero-extended the same way.
    • Replace the corr_out <= corr_out_tmp; assignment at line 1856 with corr_out <= p_re[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]; (keeping it inside the new_data branch).
  3. Append the dspcplx_mult_re instantiation only; do not add a definition of it to stereovision1.v (the model is supplied in sim_reference/). Leave every other module, including wrapper_norm, sh_reg and the dividers, untouched.

sh_reg_w is 8 for every instance of both modules (the parameter is never overridden at any instantiation site — lines 1247-1267, 1590-1610, 1734-1739), so the 10-bit zero extension and the [15:0] slice are the concrete widths.

The rewrite needs no assumption about the design's inputs or use: it is exact for all 8-bit operand values and for every value of new_data.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (dspcplx_mult_re.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll read the source to see what else might be improved.

REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78, corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr_out_tmp in corr (line 1385) — the sum lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg, which the block's M stage computes directly
BLOCKS: 64 (one per instance of corr)
MAPPING: per corr instance — CLK = clk, A_RE = {10'b0, in_l_re}, A_IM = {10'b0, in_l_im}, B_RE = {10'b0, in_r_re}, B_IM = {10'b0, in_r_im}, CONJUGATE_A = 1'b1, CONJUGATE_B = 1'b0, OPMODE_RE = 9'b000000101, RSTM_RE = 1'b0, RSTP_RE = 1'b0, CEM_RE = 1'b1, CEP_RE = 1'b1, P_RE[15:0] replaces corr_out_tmp.
PLAN:

What changes from the kept candidate. This is the kept rewrite with the corr_seq half dropped: 64 blocks instead of 76, corr_seq left exactly as written in the source. Reasoning from the measurement: the kept run is at the block's delay floor (its worst path is 0.060 clk-to-Q + 2.148 wire + 2.140 block + 0.019 setup = 4.367 at seed 1), so no further delay is available, and the only remaining lever is DSP area — the block costs a whole tile for 2 multiplies where the soft mult_9x9_fixed_pt packing gets 4. Converting an instance therefore adds area (one tile per instance against half a tile of soft multiplier plus ~1.5 CLB of adder/registers saved), and only pays where it removes a critical path.

The critical path the measurement shows ends at B_IM, i.e. the right-hand operand, which in corr is out_r1/out_r2 from the single inst_sh_reg_r_1 shared by 21 corr instances (lines 1225, 1247-1267 of wrapper_corr_20, and the same shape in wrapper_corr_10). Those nets are the long ones, and all 64 corr instances carry them, so every corr instance must be converted to hold the 4.47 ns period. corr_seq's multiplies (lines 1867-1868) read in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg — registers private to each instance (declared 1840-1843, loaded 1852-1855), fanout one multiply plus their own hold path — so their soft multiply paths are short and local and are not expected to set the period; their 24 multiplies pack into ~6-7 tiles instead of 12, which is the area this pass recovers (about 5 net DSP tiles).

Candidate regions, converted and left.

  • corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78 and corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79: converted, both into one block per instance (64 instances, 64 blocks).
  • corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104 and corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105: left, not chosen this pass. There is no correctness obstacle (the kept candidate converted them and matched); they are left because a block per corr_seq instance costs a full DSP tile for 2 multiplies while their soft form packs 4 per tile, and their paths are instance-local and not expected to be critical.

Why the block fits corr. With CONJUGATE_A = 1 and CONJUGATE_B = 0 the M stage computes A_RE*B_RE - (-A_IM)*B_IM = A_RE*B_RE + A_IM*B_IM, exactly in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im — both multiplies of lines 1374-1375 and the adder of line 1385 in one block. No term is left outside.

Visibility check (unchanged from the kept candidate, re-verified). In corr, lrexrre_reg (1367) and limxrim_reg (1368) are read only at line 1385; corr_out_tmp (1369) only at line 1379. Nothing else in the module or outside it reads them, so all three may live inside the block. corr_out (1362) is the module output, read by the wrapper (corr_out_N_tmp, lines 1247-1267 and 1273-1293), so it stays a soft register. The four same-named wires at lines 1363-1366 are undriven and unread; leave them alone.

Arithmetic check. All four operands are 8-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18 bits, so the top bit of each port is 0 as the block requires (8 ≤ 17). Each product is at most 65025; the original keeps each in a 16-bit register without truncation and truncates only their sum into the 16-bit corr_out_tmp. The block computes the sum exactly in 58 bits, so P_RE[15:0] equals (in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im) mod 2^16, bit-identical to the original corr_out_tmp. P_RE never wraps.

Registers and reset. corr has no reset, so RSTM_RE = RSTP_RE = 0. Both of the absorbed stages run unconditionally in the original (lines 1374, 1375 and 1385 sit outside the new_data branch), so CEM_RE = CEP_RE = 1 and OPMODE_RE is the constant 9'b000000101 (multiply) every cycle. Original registers start at 0 and the block's M and P_RE start at 0, so the first two cycles match too. corr_out keeps its soft register enabled by new_data.

Register stages, before and after (converted region only). corr, before: operands → stage 1 lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (1374-1375) → stage 2 corr_out_tmp (1385) → stage 3 corr_out (1379, enabled by new_data). After: operands → stage 1 block M → stage 2 block P_RE (= corr_out_tmp) → stage 3 soft corr_out. Three stages before and after; the block's 2-edge operand-to-P_RE latency matches the two stages it absorbs. corr_seq keeps its four stages unchanged (inputs → in_*_reg → lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg → corr_out_tmp → corr_out).

Edits to make (do not change anything else).

  1. In module corr (lines 1352-1387):

    • Delete the declarations of lrexrre_reg (1367), limxrim_reg (1368) and corr_out_tmp (1369), and delete lines 1374, 1375 and 1385.

    • Add wire[57:0] p_re; and the instance:

       dspcplx_mult_re u_cplx (
     .CLK(clk),

     .A_IM({{(18 - sh_reg_w){1'b0}}, in_l_im}),

    • The remaining always block keeps only the new_data branch, with corr_out <= p_re[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0]; on the taken branch and corr_out <= corr_out; otherwise.
    • This is byte-for-byte the corr half of the kept candidate's diff, which already matched the reference simulation.
  2. Leave module corr_seq (lines 1829-1871) exactly as in the unmodified source. Apply none of the kept candidate's corr_seq edits.
  3. Instantiate dspcplx_mult_re only; do not add a definition of it to stereovision1.v (the model is in sim_reference/). Leave every other module — wrapper_corr_20, wrapper_corr_10, wrapper_corr_5_seq, sh_reg, wrapper_norm*, the dividers — untouched.

sh_reg_w is 8 for every corr instance (never overridden at lines 1247-1267 and 1590-1610), so the extension is 10 bits of zero and the slice is [15:0].

The rewrite needs no assumption about the design's inputs or use: it is exact for all 8-bit operand values and for every value of new_data.

DESIGN_CHANGE: The route into B_IM/B_RE is the period-setting element (2.148 ns of wire against 2.140 ns of block). It comes from the one inst_sh_reg_r_1 whose out_r1/out_r2 feed 21 corr instances in wrapper_corr_20 (11 in wrapper_corr_10). Replicating that shift-register stage in the wrapper — several identical sh_reg instances fed by the same d_r_1/d_r_2 and wen, each serving a few corr instances — computes the same values and would let each copy be placed next to its group of DSP tiles, cutting that wire. I did not do it in the rewrite: the replicas are registers added in soft logic, so they power up unknown while the original sh_reg outputs start at 0, and sh_reg has no reset to clear them.

Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dspcplx_mult_re (precision INT18)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSPCPLX block in one configuration, "18 x 18 Complex MULT/MACC, real half (DSP_MODE = CINT18)" (AM004 v1.2.1 Table 6 (p43), chapter 5 (p76-85), register combinations Tables 22 and 23 (p83-85)). It is not the whole DSPCPLX: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (C_RE = 0, ALUMODE_RE = 0000, CARRYINSEL_RE = 000, CARRYIN_RE = 0, ASYNC_RST = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one tile holds one half of the unit; AMD's unit is two DSP58s that receive the operands once; the C_RE port is not exposed; OPMODE_RE is restricted to the listed values; the cascade, carry and pattern-detect outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P_RE = A_RE*B_RE - A_IM*B_IM; in full, with ai = CONJUGATE_A ? -A_IM : A_IM and bi = CONJUGATE_B ? -B_IM : B_IM, M <= A_RE*B_RE - ai*bi. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE_RE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A_RE[18], A_IM[18], B_RE[18], B_IM[18], CONJUGATE_A[1], CONJUGATE_B[1], OPMODE_RE[9], RSTM_RE[1], RSTP_RE[1], CEM_RE[1], CEP_RE[1]
  • output ports: P_RE[58]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each 18-bit operand must be zero (AM004 p62, as for every DSP58 multiplier input). So an unsigned operand may be at most 17 bits on A_RE, 17 bits on A_IM, 17 bits on B_RE and 17 bits on B_IM; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. P_RE is a 58-bit value accumulated modulo 2^58. The result wraps at 58 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 58 bits (modulo 2^58), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. M loads the real part of a*b from the operand and CONJUGATE pins at a clock edge where CEM_RE is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTM_RE. P_RE loads the value OPMODE_RE selects from M and P_RE at a clock edge where CEP_RE is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP_RE; the output P_RE is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A_RE, A_IM, B_RE, B_IM, CONJUGATE_A and CONJUGATE_B reach P_RE 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P_RE after edge k+1); OPMODE_RE has no register before the one that drives P_RE: the value present at a clock edge is in P_RE after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE_RE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P_RE. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P_RE <= M
    • 000100101 (accumulate): P_RE <= P_RE + M
    • 010000000 (hold): P_RE <= P_RE
    • 000000000 (zero): P_RE <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P_RE under it leaves P_RE unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • control CONJUGATE_A: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts with the operands, at the clock edge that loads M. 1 negates A_IM before the multiply (a = A_RE - j A_IM). 0 and 1 are both legal.
  • control CONJUGATE_B: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts with the operands, at the clock edge that loads M. 1 negates B_IM before the multiply (b = B_RE - j B_IM). 0 and 1 are both legal.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTM_RE and RSTP_RE; clock enables CEM_RE and CEP_RE): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P_RE is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE_RE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at RSTM_RE, RSTP_RE, CEM_RE and CEP_RE: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at A_RE, A_IM, B_RE, B_IM, CONJUGATE_A, CONJUGATE_B and OPMODE_RE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P_RE (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P_RE (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into A_RE, A_IM, B_RE, B_IM, CONJUGATE_A, CONJUGATE_B and OPMODE_RE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P_RE, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is stereovision1.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

Multiplies in the unmodified stereovision1, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 152 on 41 DSP tiles, all in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode (35 tiles with 4, 2 with 3, 2 with 2 and 2 with 1). The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle controls, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTM_RE and RSTP_RE), clock enables (CEM_RE and CEP_RE) and per-cycle controls (OPMODE_RE, CONJUGATE_A and CONJUGATE_B) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.

BLOCKS: 76 (one per instance: 64 of corr, 12 of corr_seq)
MAPPING: per corr instance — A_RE = {10'b0, in_l_re}, B_RE = {10'b0, in_r_re}, A_IM = {10'b0, in_l_im}, B_IM = {10'b0, in_r_im}, CONJUGATE_A = 1'b1, CONJUGATE_B = 1'b0, OPMODE_RE = 9'b000000101, CEM_RE = 1'b1, CEP_RE = 1'b1, RSTM_RE = 1'b0, RSTP_RE = 1'b0, CLK = clk, P_RE[15:0] replaces corr_out_tmp. Per corr_seq instance — identical, but the operands are the existing soft registers: A_RE = {10'b0, in_l_re_reg}, B_RE = {10'b0, in_r_re_reg}, A_IM = {10'b0, in_l_im_reg}, B_IM = {10'b0, in_r_im_reg}, CLK = clk, P_RE[15:0] replaces corr_out_tmp.
PLAN:

Why the block fits. With CONJUGATE_A = 1 and CONJUGATE_B = 0 the block's M stage computes A_RE*B_RE - (-A_IM)*B_IM = A_RE*B_RE + A_IM*B_IM, which is exactly in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im — the whole reduction that corr (lines 1374, 1375, 1385) and corr_seq (lines 1867, 1868, 1869) perform. Both multiplies of a module and the adder that sums them collapse into one block, so each of the 76 module instances needs exactly one block.

Visibility check. In corr, lrexrre_reg (1367) and limxrim_reg (1368) are read only at line 1385, and corr_out_tmp (1369) only at line 1379. In corr_seq, lrexrre_reg (1844) and limxrim_reg (1845) are read only at line 1869, corr_out_tmp (1846) only at line 1856. Nothing else inside or outside these modules reads them, so they may all live inside the block. corr_out is a module output and stays in soft logic. in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg in corr_seq (1840-1843) are read at 1860-1863 (their own hold path) besides the multiplies, so they stay in soft logic exactly as written and only feed the block's operand pins. (The four same-named wires in corr, lines 1363-1366, are undriven and unread; leave them alone.)

Arithmetic check. All four operands are 8-bit unsigned, so they are zero-extended to 18 bits and the top bit is 0 as the block requires (8 ≤ 17). Each original product is at most 65025 and is stored in a 16-bit register without truncation; their sum is then truncated to 16 bits by corr_out_tmp. The block computes the sum exactly in 58 bits, so P_RE[15:0] equals (in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im) mod 2^16 — bit-identical to the original corr_out_tmp. No wrap of P_RE occurs. No term is dropped: both multiplies and the add are block terms; nothing is left outside.

Registers and reset. Neither module has a reset, so RSTM_RE = RSTP_RE = 0; both pipeline stages run unconditionally in the original, so CEM_RE = CEP_RE = 1 and OPMODE_RE is the constant 9'b000000101 (multiply). Original registers start at 0 and the block's M and P_RE start at 0, so cycles 1 and 2 after power-up match too (original: corr_out_tmp loads 0+0 = 0 at the first edge; block: P_RE loads M = 0). corr_out keeps its soft enabled register driven by new_data.

Register stages, before and after.

  • corr (candidates :1374$78 and :1375$79), before: operands → stage 1 lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg → stage 2 corr_out_tmp → stage 3 corr_out (enabled by new_data). After: operands → stage 1 block M → stage 2 block P_RE (= corr_out_tmp) → stage 3 soft corr_out. Three stages before and after; the block's 2-cycle operand-to-P_RE latency matches the two stages it absorbs.
  • corr_seq (candidates :1867$104 and :1868$105), before: inputs → stage 1 in_*_reg (enabled by new_data) → stage 2 lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg → stage 3 corr_out_tmp → stage 4 corr_out. After: inputs → stage 1 soft in_*_reg (unchanged) → stage 2 block M → stage 3 block P_RE (= corr_out_tmp) → stage 4 soft corr_out. Four stages before and after.

No candidate region is left unconverted; all four are converted.

Edits to make (do not change anything else).

  1. In module corr (lines 1352-1387):

    • Delete the declarations of lrexrre_reg (1367), limxrim_reg (1368) and corr_out_tmp (1369), and delete lines 1374, 1375 and 1385.

    • Add wire [57:0] p_re; and the instance:

       dspcplx_mult_re u_cplx (
     .CLK(clk),
     .A_RE({{(18-sh_reg_w){1'b0}}, in_l_re}),
     .A_IM({{(18-sh_reg_w){1'b0}}, in_l_im}),
     .B_RE({{(18-sh_reg_w){1'b0}}, in_r_re}),
     .B_IM({{(18-sh_reg_w){1'b0}}, in_r_im}),
     .CONJUGATE_A(1'b1), .CONJUGATE_B(1'b0),
     .OPMODE_RE(9'b000000101),
     .RSTM_RE(1'b0), .RSTP_RE(1'b0),
     .CEM_RE(1'b1), .CEP_RE(1'b1),
     .P_RE(p_re));
    • The remaining always block keeps only the new_data branch, with corr_out <= p_re[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]; on the taken branch and corr_out <= corr_out; otherwise.

  2. In module corr_seq (lines 1829-1871):

    • Keep lines 1850-1865 exactly as they are (the four enabled operand registers and the corr_out load).
    • Delete the declarations of lrexrre_reg (1844), limxrim_reg (1845) and corr_out_tmp (1846), and delete lines 1867, 1868 and 1869.
    • Add wire [57:0] p_re; and the same instance, with A_RE/A_IM/B_RE/B_IM driven by in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg zero-extended the same way.
    • Replace the corr_out <= corr_out_tmp; assignment at line 1856 with corr_out <= p_re[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]; (keeping it inside the new_data branch).
  3. Append the dspcplx_mult_re instantiation only; do not add a definition of it to stereovision1.v (the model is supplied in sim_reference/). Leave every other module, including wrapper_norm, sh_reg and the dividers, untouched.

sh_reg_w is 8 for every instance of both modules (the parameter is never overridden at any instantiation site — lines 1247-1267, 1590-1610, 1734-1739), so the 10-bit zero extension and the [15:0] slice are the concrete widths.

The rewrite needs no assumption about the design's inputs or use: it is exact for all 8-bit operand values and for every value of new_data.

The plan

I'll read the source to see what else might be improved.

REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78, corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr_out_tmp in corr (line 1385) — the sum lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg, which the block's M stage computes directly
BLOCKS: 64 (one per instance of corr)
MAPPING: per corr instance — CLK = clk, A_RE = {10'b0, in_l_re}, A_IM = {10'b0, in_l_im}, B_RE = {10'b0, in_r_re}, B_IM = {10'b0, in_r_im}, CONJUGATE_A = 1'b1, CONJUGATE_B = 1'b0, OPMODE_RE = 9'b000000101, RSTM_RE = 1'b0, RSTP_RE = 1'b0, CEM_RE = 1'b1, CEP_RE = 1'b1, P_RE[15:0] replaces corr_out_tmp.
PLAN:

What changes from the kept candidate. This is the kept rewrite with the corr_seq half dropped: 64 blocks instead of 76, corr_seq left exactly as written in the source. Reasoning from the measurement: the kept run is at the block's delay floor (its worst path is 0.060 clk-to-Q + 2.148 wire + 2.140 block + 0.019 setup = 4.367 at seed 1), so no further delay is available, and the only remaining lever is DSP area — the block costs a whole tile for 2 multiplies where the soft mult_9x9_fixed_pt packing gets 4. Converting an instance therefore adds area (one tile per instance against half a tile of soft multiplier plus ~1.5 CLB of adder/registers saved), and only pays where it removes a critical path.

The critical path the measurement shows ends at B_IM, i.e. the right-hand operand, which in corr is out_r1/out_r2 from the single inst_sh_reg_r_1 shared by 21 corr instances (lines 1225, 1247-1267 of wrapper_corr_20, and the same shape in wrapper_corr_10). Those nets are the long ones, and all 64 corr instances carry them, so every corr instance must be converted to hold the 4.47 ns period. corr_seq's multiplies (lines 1867-1868) read in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg — registers private to each instance (declared 1840-1843, loaded 1852-1855), fanout one multiply plus their own hold path — so their soft multiply paths are short and local and are not expected to set the period; their 24 multiplies pack into ~6-7 tiles instead of 12, which is the area this pass recovers (about 5 net DSP tiles).

Candidate regions, converted and left.

  • corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78 and corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79: converted, both into one block per instance (64 instances, 64 blocks).
  • corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104 and corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105: left, not chosen this pass. There is no correctness obstacle (the kept candidate converted them and matched); they are left because a block per corr_seq instance costs a full DSP tile for 2 multiplies while their soft form packs 4 per tile, and their paths are instance-local and not expected to be critical.

Why the block fits corr. With CONJUGATE_A = 1 and CONJUGATE_B = 0 the M stage computes A_RE*B_RE - (-A_IM)*B_IM = A_RE*B_RE + A_IM*B_IM, exactly in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im — both multiplies of lines 1374-1375 and the adder of line 1385 in one block. No term is left outside.

Visibility check (unchanged from the kept candidate, re-verified). In corr, lrexrre_reg (1367) and limxrim_reg (1368) are read only at line 1385; corr_out_tmp (1369) only at line 1379. Nothing else in the module or outside it reads them, so all three may live inside the block. corr_out (1362) is the module output, read by the wrapper (corr_out_N_tmp, lines 1247-1267 and 1273-1293), so it stays a soft register. The four same-named wires at lines 1363-1366 are undriven and unread; leave them alone.

Arithmetic check. All four operands are 8-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18 bits, so the top bit of each port is 0 as the block requires (8 ≤ 17). Each product is at most 65025; the original keeps each in a 16-bit register without truncation and truncates only their sum into the 16-bit corr_out_tmp. The block computes the sum exactly in 58 bits, so P_RE[15:0] equals (in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im) mod 2^16, bit-identical to the original corr_out_tmp. P_RE never wraps.

Registers and reset. corr has no reset, so RSTM_RE = RSTP_RE = 0. Both of the absorbed stages run unconditionally in the original (lines 1374, 1375 and 1385 sit outside the new_data branch), so CEM_RE = CEP_RE = 1 and OPMODE_RE is the constant 9'b000000101 (multiply) every cycle. Original registers start at 0 and the block's M and P_RE start at 0, so the first two cycles match too. corr_out keeps its soft register enabled by new_data.

Register stages, before and after (converted region only). corr, before: operands → stage 1 lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (1374-1375) → stage 2 corr_out_tmp (1385) → stage 3 corr_out (1379, enabled by new_data). After: operands → stage 1 block M → stage 2 block P_RE (= corr_out_tmp) → stage 3 soft corr_out. Three stages before and after; the block's 2-edge operand-to-P_RE latency matches the two stages it absorbs. corr_seq keeps its four stages unchanged (inputs → in_*_reg → lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg → corr_out_tmp → corr_out).

Edits to make (do not change anything else).

  1. In module corr (lines 1352-1387):

    • Delete the declarations of lrexrre_reg (1367), limxrim_reg (1368) and corr_out_tmp (1369), and delete lines 1374, 1375 and 1385.

    • Add wire[57:0] p_re; and the instance:

       dspcplx_mult_re u_cplx (

     .B_RE({{(18 - sh_reg_w){1'b0}}, in_r_re}),

    • The remaining always block keeps only the new_data branch, with corr_out <= p_re[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0]; on the taken branch and corr_out <= corr_out; otherwise.
    • This is byte-for-byte the corr half of the kept candidate's diff, which already matched the reference simulation.
  2. Leave module corr_seq (lines 1829-1871) exactly as in the unmodified source. Apply none of the kept candidate's corr_seq edits.
  3. Instantiate dspcplx_mult_re only; do not add a definition of it to stereovision1.v (the model is in sim_reference/). Leave every other module — wrapper_corr_20, wrapper_corr_10, wrapper_corr_5_seq, sh_reg, wrapper_norm*, the dividers — untouched.

sh_reg_w is 8 for every corr instance (never overridden at lines 1247-1267 and 1590-1610), so the extension is 10 bits of zero and the slice is [15:0].

The rewrite needs no assumption about the design's inputs or use: it is exact for all 8-bit operand values and for every value of new_data.

DESIGN_CHANGE: The route into B_IM/B_RE is the period-setting element (2.148 ns of wire against 2.140 ns of block). It comes from the one inst_sh_reg_r_1 whose out_r1/out_r2 feed 21 corr instances in wrapper_corr_20 (11 in wrapper_corr_10). Replicating that shift-register stage in the wrapper — several identical sh_reg instances fed by the same d_r_1/d_r_2 and wen, each serving a few corr instances — computes the same values and would let each copy be placed next to its group of DSP tiles, cutting that wire. I did not do it in the rewrite: the replicas are registers added in soft logic, so they power up unknown while the original sh_reg outputs start at 0, and sh_reg has no reset to clear them.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (dspcplx_mult_re.v) are in sim_reference/.

  • Candidate regions. Task

Edit stereovision1.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dspcplx_mult_re (precision INT18)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSPCPLX block in one configuration, "18 x 18 Complex MULT/MACC, real half (DSP_MODE = CINT18)" (AM004 v1.2.1 Table 6 (p43), chapter 5 (p76-85), register combinations Tables 22 and 23 (p83-85)). It is not the whole DSPCPLX: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (C_RE = 0, ALUMODE_RE = 0000, CARRYINSEL_RE = 000, CARRYIN_RE = 0, ASYNC_RST = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one tile holds one half of the unit; AMD's unit is two DSP58s that receive the operands once; the C_RE port is not exposed; OPMODE_RE is restricted to the listed values; the cascade, carry and pattern-detect outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P_RE = A_RE*B_RE - A_IM*B_IM; in full, with ai = CONJUGATE_A ? -A_IM : A_IM and bi = CONJUGATE_B ? -B_IM : B_IM, M <= A_RE*B_RE - ai*bi. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE_RE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A_RE[18], A_IM[18], B_RE[18], B_IM[18], CONJUGATE_A[1], CONJUGATE_B[1], OPMODE_RE[9], RSTM_RE[1], RSTP_RE[1], CEM_RE[1], CEP_RE[1]
  • output ports: P_RE[58]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each 18-bit operand must be zero (AM004 p62, as for every DSP58 multiplier input). So an unsigned operand may be at most 17 bits on A_RE, 17 bits on A_IM, 17 bits on B_RE and 17 bits on B_IM; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. P_RE is a 58-bit value accumulated modulo 2^58. The result wraps at 58 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 58 bits (modulo 2^58), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. M loads the real part of a*b from the operand and CONJUGATE pins at a clock edge where CEM_RE is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTM_RE. P_RE loads the value OPMODE_RE selects from M and P_RE at a clock edge where CEP_RE is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP_RE; the output P_RE is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A_RE, A_IM, B_RE, B_IM, CONJUGATE_A and CONJUGATE_B reach P_RE 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P_RE after edge k+1); OPMODE_RE has no register before the one that drives P_RE: the value present at a clock edge is in P_RE after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE_RE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P_RE. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P_RE <= M
    • 000100101 (accumulate): P_RE <= P_RE + M
    • 010000000 (hold): P_RE <= P_RE
    • 000000000 (zero): P_RE <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P_RE under it leaves P_RE unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • control CONJUGATE_A: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts with the operands, at the clock edge that loads M. 1 negates A_IM before the multiply (a = A_RE - j A_IM). 0 and 1 are both legal.
  • control CONJUGATE_B: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts with the operands, at the clock edge that loads M. 1 negates B_IM before the multiply (b = B_RE - j B_IM). 0 and 1 are both legal.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTM_RE and RSTP_RE; clock enables CEM_RE and CEP_RE): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P_RE is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE_RE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at RSTM_RE, RSTP_RE, CEM_RE and CEP_RE: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at A_RE, A_IM, B_RE, B_IM, CONJUGATE_A, CONJUGATE_B and OPMODE_RE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P_RE (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P_RE (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into A_RE, A_IM, B_RE, B_IM, CONJUGATE_A, CONJUGATE_B and OPMODE_RE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P_RE, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Multiplies in the approved plan: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78, corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79. The plan names their shared sum as 'corr_out_tmp in corr (line 1385) — the sum lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg, which the block's M stage computes directly'.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle controls, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTM_RE and RSTP_RE), clock enables (CEM_RE and CEP_RE) and per-cycle controls (OPMODE_RE, CONJUGATE_A and CONJUGATE_B) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh stereovision1.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the source to see what else might be improved.

REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78, corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr_out_tmp in corr (line 1385) — the sum lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg, which the block's M stage computes directly
BLOCKS: 64 (one per instance of corr)
MAPPING: per corr instance — CLK = clk, A_RE = {10'b0, in_l_re}, A_IM = {10'b0, in_l_im}, B_RE = {10'b0, in_r_re}, B_IM = {10'b0, in_r_im}, CONJUGATE_A = 1'b1, CONJUGATE_B = 1'b0, OPMODE_RE = 9'b000000101, RSTM_RE = 1'b0, RSTP_RE = 1'b0, CEM_RE = 1'b1, CEP_RE = 1'b1, P_RE[15:0] replaces corr_out_tmp.
PLAN:

What changes from the kept candidate. This is the kept rewrite with the corr_seq half dropped: 64 blocks instead of 76, corr_seq left exactly as written in the source. Reasoning from the measurement: the kept run is at the block's delay floor (its worst path is 0.060 clk-to-Q + 2.148 wire + 2.140 block + 0.019 setup = 4.367 at seed 1), so no further delay is available, and the only remaining lever is DSP area — the block costs a whole tile for 2 multiplies where the soft mult_9x9_fixed_pt packing gets 4. Converting an instance therefore adds area (one tile per instance against half a tile of soft multiplier plus ~1.5 CLB of adder/registers saved), and only pays where it removes a critical path.

The critical path the measurement shows ends at B_IM, i.e. the right-hand operand, which in corr is out_r1/out_r2 from the single inst_sh_reg_r_1 shared by 21 corr instances (lines 1225, 1247-1267 of wrapper_corr_20, and the same shape in wrapper_corr_10). Those nets are the long ones, and all 64 corr instances carry them, so every corr instance must be converted to hold the 4.47 ns period. corr_seq's multiplies (lines 1867-1868) read in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg — registers private to each instance (declared 1840-1843, loaded 1852-1855), fanout one multiply plus their own hold path — so their soft multiply paths are short and local and are not expected to set the period; their 24 multiplies pack into ~6-7 tiles instead of 12, which is the area this pass recovers (about 5 net DSP tiles).

Candidate regions, converted and left.

  • corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78 and corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79: converted, both into one block per instance (64 instances, 64 blocks).
  • corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104 and corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105: left, not chosen this pass. There is no correctness obstacle (the kept candidate converted them and matched); they are left because a block per corr_seq instance costs a full DSP tile for 2 multiplies while their soft form packs 4 per tile, and their paths are instance-local and not expected to be critical.

Why the block fits corr. With CONJUGATE_A = 1 and CONJUGATE_B = 0 the M stage computes A_RE*B_RE - (-A_IM)*B_IM = A_RE*B_RE + A_IM*B_IM, exactly in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im — both multiplies of lines 1374-1375 and the adder of line 1385 in one block. No term is left outside.

Visibility check (unchanged from the kept candidate, re-verified). In corr, lrexrre_reg (1367) and limxrim_reg (1368) are read only at line 1385; corr_out_tmp (1369) only at line 1379. Nothing else in the module or outside it reads them, so all three may live inside the block. corr_out (1362) is the module output, read by the wrapper (corr_out_N_tmp, lines 1247-1267 and 1273-1293), so it stays a soft register. The four same-named wires at lines 1363-1366 are undriven and unread; leave them alone.

Arithmetic check. All four operands are 8-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18 bits, so the top bit of each port is 0 as the block requires (8 ≤ 17). Each product is at most 65025; the original keeps each in a 16-bit register without truncation and truncates only their sum into the 16-bit corr_out_tmp. The block computes the sum exactly in 58 bits, so P_RE[15:0] equals (in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im) mod 2^16, bit-identical to the original corr_out_tmp. P_RE never wraps.

Registers and reset. corr has no reset, so RSTM_RE = RSTP_RE = 0. Both of the absorbed stages run unconditionally in the original (lines 1374, 1375 and 1385 sit outside the new_data branch), so CEM_RE = CEP_RE = 1 and OPMODE_RE is the constant 9'b000000101 (multiply) every cycle. Original registers start at 0 and the block's M and P_RE start at 0, so the first two cycles match too. corr_out keeps its soft register enabled by new_data.

Register stages, before and after (converted region only). corr, before: operands → stage 1 lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (1374-1375) → stage 2 corr_out_tmp (1385) → stage 3 corr_out (1379, enabled by new_data). After: operands → stage 1 block M → stage 2 block P_RE (= corr_out_tmp) → stage 3 soft corr_out. Three stages before and after; the block's 2-edge operand-to-P_RE latency matches the two stages it absorbs. corr_seq keeps its four stages unchanged (inputs → in_*_reg → lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg → corr_out_tmp → corr_out).

Edits to make (do not change anything else).

  1. In module corr (lines 1352-1387):

    • Delete the declarations of lrexrre_reg (1367), limxrim_reg (1368) and corr_out_tmp (1369), and delete lines 1374, 1375 and 1385.

    • Add wire[57:0] p_re; and the instance:

    • This is byte-for-byte the corr half of the kept candidate's diff, which already matched the reference simulation.
  2. Leave module corr_seq (lines 1829-1871) exactly as in the unmodified source. Apply none of the kept candidate's corr_seq edits.

sh_reg_w is 8 for every corr instance (never overridden at lines 1247-1267 and 1590-1610), so the extension is 10 bits of zero and the slice is [15:0].

The rewrite needs no assumption about the design's inputs or use: it is exact for all 8-bit operand values and for every value of new_data.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (dspcplx_mult_re.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

1	// dspcplx_mult_re: behavioural model of the arch mode dspcplx_mult_re_mode, the real half of the AMD
2	// DSPCPLX unit (two DSP58s with DSP_MODE = CINT18; AM004 v1.2.1 chapter 5, "18 x 18 Complex MULT/MACC"),
3	// attributes AREG = BREG = ACASCREG = BCASCREG = ADREG = 0, MREG = 1, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0,
4	// CONJUGATEREG_A = CONJUGATEREG_B = 0, ALUMODE = 0000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, C unused. This
5	// mode is one tile holding this half alone; it takes all four operands, as AMD's unit routes them to
6	// both halves. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
7	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSPCPLX.v with the DSP_* atoms it instantiates from the
8	// same directory (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the atoms' initial blocks: DSP_M_DATA58.v
9	// line 156, DSP_OUTPUT58.v line 197, DSP_PREADD_DATA58.v line 309), read in place and never copied.
10	// With a = A_RE + j*A_IM (A_IM negated when CONJUGATE_A = 1) and b = B_RE + j*B_IM (B_IM negated when
11	// CONJUGATE_B = 1), the real part of a*b is ar*br - ai*bi.
12	// Per clock edge: M <= that value if CEM_RE (cleared by RSTM_RE); P_RE <= f(OPMODE_RE, M, P_RE) if CEP_RE
13	// (cleared by RSTP_RE). Operands and CONJUGATE_A/B to P_RE: 2 cycles; OPMODE_RE to P_RE: 1 cycle. P_RE wraps
14	// at 58 bits. OPMODE_RE is restricted to the four values below; on any other value at an edge that
15	// loads P_RE, P_RE becomes X.
16	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
17	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
18	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
19	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
20	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
21	module dspcplx_mult_re (
22	    input CLK,
23	    input [17:0] A_RE,
24	    input [17:0] A_IM,
25	    input [17:0] B_RE,
26	    input [17:0] B_IM,
27	    input CONJUGATE_A,
28	    input CONJUGATE_B,
29	    input [8:0] OPMODE_RE,
30	    input RSTM_RE, RSTP_RE, CEM_RE, CEP_RE,
31	    output [57:0] P_RE
32	);
33	    wire signed [18:0] ar = $signed(A_RE);
34	    wire signed [18:0] br = $signed(B_RE);
35	    wire signed [18:0] ai = CONJUGATE_A ? -$signed({A_IM[17], A_IM}) : $signed({A_IM[17], A_IM});
36	    wire signed [18:0] bi = CONJUGATE_B ? -$signed({B_IM[17], B_IM}) : $signed({B_IM[17], B_IM});
37	    wire signed [38:0] val = ar*br - ai*bi;                    // exact
38	    reg signed [38:0] m = 0;                                   // the M register
39	    reg [57:0] p = 0;
40	    wire [57:0] mx = {{19{m[38]}}, m};
41	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
42	    function [57:0] alu;
43	        input [8:0] opmode;
44	        input [57:0] p;
45	        input [57:0] mx;
46	        case (opmode)
47	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
48	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // accumulate (Z = P)
49	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
50	            9'b000000000: alu = 58'd0;         // zero
51	            default:      alu = {58{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
52	        endcase
53	    endfunction
54	    wire [57:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE_RE, p, mx);
55	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
56	        if (RSTM_RE) m <= 0; else if (CEM_RE) m <= val;
57	        if (RSTP_RE) p <= 0; else if (CEP_RE) p <= nxt;
58	    end
59	    assign P_RE = p;
60	endmodule

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dspcplx_mult_re (precision INT18)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSPCPLX block in one configuration, "18 x 18 Complex MULT/MACC, real half (DSP_MODE = CINT18)" (AM004 v1.2.1 Table 6 (p43), chapter 5 (p76-85), register combinations Tables 22 and 23 (p83-85)). It is not the whole DSPCPLX: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (C_RE = 0, ALUMODE_RE = 0000, CARRYINSEL_RE = 000, CARRYIN_RE = 0, ASYNC_RST = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one tile holds one half of the unit; AMD's unit is two DSP58s that receive the operands once; the C_RE port is not exposed; OPMODE_RE is restricted to the listed values; the cascade, carry and pattern-detect outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P_RE = A_RE*B_RE - A_IM*B_IM; in full, with ai = CONJUGATE_A ? -A_IM : A_IM and bi = CONJUGATE_B ? -B_IM : B_IM, M <= A_RE*B_RE - ai*bi. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE_RE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A_RE[18], A_IM[18], B_RE[18], B_IM[18], CONJUGATE_A[1], CONJUGATE_B[1], OPMODE_RE[9], RSTM_RE[1], RSTP_RE[1], CEM_RE[1], CEP_RE[1]
  • output ports: P_RE[58]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each 18-bit operand must be zero (AM004 p62, as for every DSP58 multiplier input). So an unsigned operand may be at most 17 bits on A_RE, 17 bits on A_IM, 17 bits on B_RE and 17 bits on B_IM; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. P_RE is a 58-bit value accumulated modulo 2^58. The result wraps at 58 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 58 bits (modulo 2^58), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. M loads the real part of a*b from the operand and CONJUGATE pins at a clock edge where CEM_RE is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTM_RE. P_RE loads the value OPMODE_RE selects from M and P_RE at a clock edge where CEP_RE is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP_RE; the output P_RE is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A_RE, A_IM, B_RE, B_IM, CONJUGATE_A and CONJUGATE_B reach P_RE 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P_RE after edge k+1); OPMODE_RE has no register before the one that drives P_RE: the value present at a clock edge is in P_RE after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE_RE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P_RE. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P_RE <= M
    • 000100101 (accumulate): P_RE <= P_RE + M
    • 010000000 (hold): P_RE <= P_RE
    • 000000000 (zero): P_RE <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P_RE under it leaves P_RE unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • control CONJUGATE_A: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts with the operands, at the clock edge that loads M. 1 negates A_IM before the multiply (a = A_RE - j A_IM). 0 and 1 are both legal.
  • control CONJUGATE_B: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts with the operands, at the clock edge that loads M. 1 negates B_IM before the multiply (b = B_RE - j B_IM). 0 and 1 are both legal.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTM_RE and RSTP_RE; clock enables CEM_RE and CEP_RE): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P_RE is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE_RE may be a constant legal value.

  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into A_RE, A_IM, B_RE, B_IM, CONJUGATE_A, CONJUGATE_B and OPMODE_RE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P_RE, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.

The design

The file stereovision1.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle controls, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTM_RE and RSTP_RE), clock enables (CEM_RE and CEP_RE) and per-cycle controls (OPMODE_RE, CONJUGATE_A and CONJUGATE_B) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

The approved plan

I'll read the source to see what else might be improved.

REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78, corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr_out_tmp in corr (line 1385) — the sum lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg, which the block's M stage computes directly

PLAN:

What changes from the kept candidate. This is the kept rewrite with the corr_seq half dropped: 64 blocks instead of 76, corr_seq left exactly as written in the source. Reasoning from the measurement: the kept run is at the block's delay floor (its worst path is 0.060 clk-to-Q + 2.148 wire + 2.140 block + 0.019 setup = 4.367 at seed 1), so no further delay is available, and the only remaining lever is DSP area — the block costs a whole tile for 2 multiplies where the soft mult_9x9_fixed_pt packing gets 4. Converting an instance therefore adds area (one tile per instance against half a tile of soft multiplier plus ~1.5 CLB of adder/registers saved), and only pays where it removes a critical path.

The critical path the measurement shows ends at B_IM, i.e. the right-hand operand, which in corr is out_r1/out_r2 from the single inst_sh_reg_r_1 shared by 21 corr instances (lines 1225, 1247-1267 of wrapper_corr_20, and the same shape in wrapper_corr_10). Those nets are the long ones, and all 64 corr instances carry them, so every corr instance must be converted to hold the 4.47 ns period. corr_seq's multiplies (lines 1867-1868) read in_l_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_r_im_reg — registers private to each instance (declared 1840-1843, loaded 1852-1855), fanout one multiply plus their own hold path — so their soft multiply paths are short and local and are not expected to set the period; their 24 multiplies pack into ~6-7 tiles instead of 12, which is the area this pass recovers (about 5 net DSP tiles).

Candidate regions, converted and left.

  • corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78 and corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79: converted, both into one block per instance (64 instances, 64 blocks).
  • corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104 and corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105: left, not chosen this pass. There is no correctness obstacle (the kept candidate converted them and matched); they are left because a block per corr_seq instance costs a full DSP tile for 2 multiplies while their soft form packs 4 per tile, and their paths are instance-local and not expected to be critical.

Why the block fits corr. With CONJUGATE_A = 1 and CONJUGATE_B = 0 the M stage computes A_RE*B_RE - (-A_IM)*B_IM = A_RE*B_RE + A_IM*B_IM, exactly in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im — both multiplies of lines 1374-1375 and the adder of line 1385 in one block. No term is left outside.

Visibility check (unchanged from the kept candidate, re-verified). In corr, lrexrre_reg (1367) and limxrim_reg (1368) are read only at line 1385; corr_out_tmp (1369) only at line 1379. Nothing else in the module or outside it reads them, so all three may live inside the block. corr_out (1362) is the module output, read by the wrapper (corr_out_N_tmp, lines 1247-1267 and 1273-1293), so it stays a soft register. The four same-named wires at lines 1363-1366 are undriven and unread; leave them alone.

Arithmetic check. All four operands are 8-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18 bits, so the top bit of each port is 0 as the block requires (8 ≤ 17). Each product is at most 65025; the original keeps each in a 16-bit register without truncation and truncates only their sum into the 16-bit corr_out_tmp. The block computes the sum exactly in 58 bits, so P_RE[15:0] equals (in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im) mod 2^16, bit-identical to the original corr_out_tmp. P_RE never wraps.

Registers and reset. corr has no reset, so RSTM_RE = RSTP_RE = 0. Both of the absorbed stages run unconditionally in the original (lines 1374, 1375 and 1385 sit outside the new_data branch), so CEM_RE = CEP_RE = 1 and OPMODE_RE is the constant 9'b000000101 (multiply) every cycle. Original registers start at 0 and the block's M and P_RE start at 0, so the first two cycles match too. corr_out keeps its soft register enabled by new_data.

Register stages, before and after (converted region only). corr, before: operands → stage 1 lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg (1374-1375) → stage 2 corr_out_tmp (1385) → stage 3 corr_out (1379, enabled by new_data). After: operands → stage 1 block M → stage 2 block P_RE (= corr_out_tmp) → stage 3 soft corr_out. Three stages before and after; the block's 2-edge operand-to-P_RE latency matches the two stages it absorbs. corr_seq keeps its four stages unchanged (inputs → in_*_reg → lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg → corr_out_tmp → corr_out).

  1. In module corr (lines 1352-1387):

sh_reg_w is 8 for every corr instance (never overridden at lines 1247-1267 and 1590-1610), so the extension is 10 bits of zero and the slice is [15:0].

The rewrite needs no assumption about the design's inputs or use: it is exact for all 8-bit operand values and for every value of new_data.

DESIGN_CHANGE: The route into B_IM/B_RE is the period-setting element (2.148 ns of wire against 2.140 ns of block). It comes from the one inst_sh_reg_r_1 whose out_r1/out_r2 feed 21 corr instances in wrapper_corr_20 (11 in wrapper_corr_10). Replicating that shift-register stage in the wrapper — several identical sh_reg instances fed by the same d_r_1/d_r_2 and wen, each serving a few corr instances — computes the same values and would let each copy be placed next to its group of DSP tiles, cutting that wire. I did not do it in the rewrite: the replicas are registers added in soft logic, so they power up unknown while the original sh_reg outputs start at 0, and sh_reg has no reset to clear them.
