ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the block contract, and the correctness rules.

Plan conformance — The diff touches only module corr (stereovision1.v:1352-1399), exactly the regions the plan names ($mul$...:1374$78, $mul$...:1375$79) plus the declared SHARED_REDUCTION adder at old line 1385. corr_seq is byte-identical to the original (stereovision1.v:1841-1883, multiplies at 1879-1880 and the adder at 1881 left soft, as the plan states). No dspcplx_mult_re definition was added to the design (stereovision1.v:1373 is the only reference); every other module is untouched. One block per corr instance; 2×21 + 2×11 = 64 instances (stereovision1.v:261,268,275,281 → 1247-1267, 1602+), matching BLOCKS: 64 and within the 306 DSP tiles.

Ports — All 12 inputs connected by name (stereovision1.v:1374-1385): CLK, A_RE, A_IM, B_RE, B_IM, CONJUGATE_A, CONJUGATE_B, OPMODE_RE, RSTM_RE, RSTP_RE, CEM_RE, CEP_RE. P_RE drives only the new wire[57:0] p_re (1367), read solely at 1392 as p_re[15:0] into corr_out, as the MAPPING says. Unneeded resets tied 0, unneeded enables tied 1, OPMODE_RE a constant legal value — as the connections rule allows.

Terms and arithmetic — With CONJUGATE_A=1, CONJUGATE_B=0 the model (sim_reference/dspcplx_mult_re.v:35-37) gives M ⇐ A_RE·B_RE + A_IM·B_IM = in_l_re*in_r_re + in_l_im*in_r_im, both original terms, nothing left outside. Positional connections at 1247-1267 confirm in_l_re/in_l_im are the A operands and in_r_re/in_r_im the B operands. Operands are 8-bit unsigned zero-extended to 18 bits ({(18-sh_reg_w){1'b0}}, sh_reg_w = 8 — no instance overrides it, no defparam anywhere), so each port's top bit is 0 as required. Products ≤ 65025 fit the original 16-bit regs untruncated; the original truncates only the sum, and p_re[15:0] is that sum mod 2^16. No 58-bit wrap.

Registers, reset, latency — M then P_RE is 2 edges, matching the two absorbed stages (lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg → corr_out_tmp); corr_out stays a soft register gated by new_data, so three stages before and after. Both absorbed stages ran unconditionally in the original, so CEM_RE = CEP_RE = 1 and OPMODE_RE = multiply every cycle is right; corr has no reset port, so RSTM_RE = RSTP_RE = 0 holds across the testbench's mid-run reset too. Block registers power up at 0, matching the original registers' zero start; no soft register was added whose initial value is relied on.

Visibility — lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg and corr_out_tmp were module-local to corr and read only by each other and by corr_out; grep confirms the surviving occurrences are all in corr_seq. corr_out remains a soft register feeding the wrapper. The four undriven *_reg wires (1363-1366) are left alone as planned.

No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is exact for all operand values and all new_data. Simulation: 6121 cycles, 0 same-cycle mismatches, 0 X (sim_run.log:12-27); the corr/corr_seq diagnostic rows are "nofile" informational, not the verdict.

1	// dspcplx_mult_re: behavioural model of the arch mode dspcplx_mult_re_mode, the real half of the AMD
2	// DSPCPLX unit (two DSP58s with DSP_MODE = CINT18; AM004 v1.2.1 chapter 5, "18 x 18 Complex MULT/MACC"),
3	// attributes AREG = BREG = ACASCREG = BCASCREG = ADREG = 0, MREG = 1, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0,
4	// CONJUGATEREG_A = CONJUGATEREG_B = 0, ALUMODE = 0000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, C unused. This
5	// mode is one tile holding this half alone; it takes all four operands, as AMD's unit routes them to
6	// both halves. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
7	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSPCPLX.v with the DSP_* atoms it instantiates from the
8	// same directory (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the atoms' initial blocks: DSP_M_DATA58.v
9	// line 156, DSP_OUTPUT58.v line 197, DSP_PREADD_DATA58.v line 309), read in place and never copied.
10	// With a = A_RE + j*A_IM (A_IM negated when CONJUGATE_A = 1) and b = B_RE + j*B_IM (B_IM negated when
11	// CONJUGATE_B = 1), the real part of a*b is ar*br - ai*bi.
12	// Per clock edge: M <= that value if CEM_RE (cleared by RSTM_RE); P_RE <= f(OPMODE_RE, M, P_RE) if CEP_RE
13	// (cleared by RSTP_RE). Operands and CONJUGATE_A/B to P_RE: 2 cycles; OPMODE_RE to P_RE: 1 cycle. P_RE wraps
14	// at 58 bits. OPMODE_RE is restricted to the four values below; on any other value at an edge that
15	// loads P_RE, P_RE becomes X.
16	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
17	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
18	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
19	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
20	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
21	module dspcplx_mult_re (
22	    input CLK,
23	    input [17:0] A_RE,
24	    input [17:0] A_IM,
25	    input [17:0] B_RE,
26	    input [17:0] B_IM,
27	    input CONJUGATE_A,
28	    input CONJUGATE_B,
29	    input [8:0] OPMODE_RE,
30	    input RSTM_RE, RSTP_RE, CEM_RE, CEP_RE,
31	    output [57:0] P_RE
32	);
33	    wire signed [18:0] ar = $signed(A_RE);
34	    wire signed [18:0] br = $signed(B_RE);
35	    wire signed [18:0] ai = CONJUGATE_A ? -$signed({A_IM[17], A_IM}) : $signed({A_IM[17], A_IM});
36	    wire signed [18:0] bi = CONJUGATE_B ? -$signed({B_IM[17], B_IM}) : $signed({B_IM[17], B_IM});
37	    wire signed [38:0] val = ar*br - ai*bi;                    // exact
38	    reg signed [38:0] m = 0;                                   // the M register
39	    reg [57:0] p = 0;
40	    wire [57:0] mx = {{19{m[38]}}, m};
41	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
42	    function [57:0] alu;
43	        input [8:0] opmode;
44	        input [57:0] p;
45	        input [57:0] mx;
46	        case (opmode)
47	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
48	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // accumulate (Z = P)
49	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
50	            9'b000000000: alu = 58'd0;         // zero
51	            default:      alu = {58{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
52	        endcase
53	    endfunction
54	    wire [57:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE_RE, p, mx);
55	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
56	        if (RSTM_RE) m <= 0; else if (CEM_RE) m <= val;
57	        if (RSTP_RE) p <= 0; else if (CEP_RE) p <= nxt;
58	    end
59	    assign P_RE = p;
60	endmodule

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: no gain beyond noise
revisions allowed

Pass 1
loop stopped by
no gain beyond noise (gain -8.23% against first, min-gain 0.0877 (baseline seed_figures (area x delay over the entry's seed runs)))
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_small_sdc · used area 2.65058e+07 · critical path 5.50859 ns · wirelength 153743 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 1.4601e+08 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 8.77%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 8.77%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

Feedback this rewrite's planner was given 4384 chars
Every candidate tried so far in this run, in order. Each row gives the candidate's figures at seed 1, which every measured candidate was measured at, and then, where more seeds were run, the median over the seeds named, which is the figure the loop compared. The period is register to register (I/O pads not timed). The product is the run's objective, area × delay; the ratios are against the baseline's figures (the median of five seeds, 1 to 5; A/A0 area, D/D0 period, and their product). The original design is the result to beat: the run holds it until a rewrite's area × delay is below the original's, and at the end keeps a rewrite only if its median over every seed is still below. It now holds the row marked "kept" (the lowest compared product, the earliest on a tie):
